本次報告：法說會

儒鴻 (1476 TT) 
不利因素消退，營運展望正向

永豐觀點 
隨著美國期中選舉結果出爐，關稅及戰爭兩大不利因素有望逐步消退，有利成衣產
業評價回升。公司訂單能見度維持六個月，新興品牌客戶訂單動能強勁，推升營收
及獲利表現，後市展望持續正向，研究部上調儒鴻目標價，維持買進投資評等。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，目標價上調至 500 元：儒鴻 3Q26 毛利率表現優於預期，反
映新興品牌客戶訂單動能強勁，改善產品組合，且原材料成本壓力舒緩，帶動獲利
表現回升。展望後市，儒鴻訂單能見度維持六個月，新興品牌客戶訂單動能持續成
長強勁，既有品牌客戶亦維持穩定回補庫存力道，營運展望正向。研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 25.39/27.79 元，上調本益比評價至 18X，依 2027 之 EPS
進行評價，目標價上調至 500 元，投資評等維持買進，依公司去年股利配發率
74.63%估算，以昨日收盤價來看，目前約具備 4.9%殖利率，應具備下檔殖利率保
護。

ESG 評析 
儒鴻企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。
SinoPac Securities Investment Service licenses and applies the SASB Materiality Map® 
General Issue Categories and Disclosure Topics in our work.

### Page 2

2

儒鴻為國內成衣業龍頭廠商：儒鴻成立於 1977 年，主要生產彈性針織布與運動服
飾，並垂直整合染整、織布到成衣製造，具有一條龍整合優勢，此外公司持續提升
布料研發和服飾設計能力，積極研發出具流行性、運動性、休閒性、環保性等功能
性之布料，廣泛應用於高級時裝、運動服、休閒服裝、內睡衣、韻律裝等多樣化產
品系列，並銷售至全球知名品牌及零售商，公司 2025 年營收結構為：成衣 66.4%、
針織布 33.6%，銷售地區以美洲為主，主要客戶包括 Nike、Lululemon、Under 
Armour 等知名大廠。 
3Q26 EPS 6.36 元，符合預期：儒鴻 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元（+3.9% QoQ，
+8.8% YoY），略優於預期，主因新興品牌客戶訂單需求強勁，推升營收表現，毛
利率 31.18%（+1.17pts. QoQ，+2.52pts. YoY），優於預期，除因新興品牌客戶
訂單增長，帶動產品組合改善外，儒鴻持續與客戶進行議價，以轉嫁原材料成本上
漲，推升毛利率表現回升，稅後淨利 17.4 億元（+14.2% QoQ，+7.3% YoY），符
合預期，其中，營業費用高於預期，主因儒鴻獲利強勁，認列季獎金費用較預期高
所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 6.36 元，符合預期。

### Page 3

3

10 月營收符合預期，4Q26 營收預估季增：儒鴻 10 月營收 34 億元，-3.1% MoM，
+10.7% YoY，符合預期。展望 4Q26，新興品牌客戶訂單動能強勁，將抵銷布料
淡季造成的營收下滑力道，預期 4Q26 營收將季增，且因產品組合持續優化，及原
材料成本陸續轉嫁，毛利率表現有望穩定回升，推升獲利表現，基此，研究部預估
4Q26 營收 106.2 億元（+0.5％ QoQ，+14.6％ YoY），毛利率 31.5%（+0.32 
pts. QoQ，+1.31 pts. YoY），稅後淨利 18.3 億元（+5.2% QoQ，+15.9％ YoY），
稅後 EPS 6.68 元。 
不利因素消退，產業評價回升，維持買進投資建議：展望後市，儒鴻訂單能見度維
持約六個月，其中，新興品牌客戶訂單動能持續成長強勁，既有品牌客戶亦維持穩
定回補庫存力道。隨著美國期中選舉結果出爐，關稅政策、美伊戰爭此些不利公司
營運及評價的兩大變數皆有望消退，儘管公司維持先前營收及毛利率指引，但隨著
大環境不確定性因素下降、產品組合持續轉佳、原物料成本下跌及向下轉嫁、垂直
整合比重提升，應有利營收及毛利率表現穩步走升，推升獲利表現。公司 2027 年
預計將藉由產能去瓶頸，提升約個位數水準，印尼新廠預期將於 4Q27-1Q28 投

### Page 4

4

產，規劃月產能為 180 萬件，將分兩期完成。綜合來看，研究部評估儒鴻訂單動
能維持穩健，隨著公司持續導入新產品及新客人加入，有利儒鴻營運表現提升，而
隨著原材料成本成功轉嫁及產品組合持續好轉，毛利率預期將穩步回升，研究部維
持正向看待儒鴻後市獲利表現。研究部預估 2026 年營收 409 億元（+7.7％YoY），
毛利率 31.21%，稅後淨利 69.7 億元（+26.3％YoY），稅後 EPS 25.39 元，預估
2027 年營收 442.3 億元（+8.2％YoY），毛利率 31.5%，稅後淨利 76.3 億元（+9.5
％YoY），稅後 EPS 27.79 元。隨著大環境不利因素消退，成衣產業評價應回升，
儒鴻過往本益比區間多位於 12-30 倍，研究部上調儒鴻本益比評價至 18X，依 2027
之 EPS 進行評價，目標價上調至 500 元，投資評等維持買進，依公司去年股利配
發率 74.63%估算，以昨日收盤價來看，目前約具備 4.9%殖利率，應具備下檔殖
利率保護。

## Document 048: 20261125【投研報告】市場快訊_AI疑慮未除，科技股震盪加劇

AI疑慮未除，科技股震盪加劇
2026年 11月 25日
一、市場表現
1. 美股走勢分歧，科技股反彈未能延續：美股 11月 24日走勢分歧，前一交易日因黃仁
勳重返輝達所帶動的科技股強勁反彈未能延續，AI 及半導體類股再度面臨沉重賣壓，
費城半導體指數重挫 7.9%，那斯達克綜合指數下跌 1.48%。相較之下，非科技類股表
現相對穩健，道瓊工業指數逆勢上漲，顯示資金仍存在產業輪動現象，而非全面撤離股
票市場。
表一：美股主要指數 11/24表現
指數 收盤 漲跌幅
道瓊工業指數 50,018.70 +0.85%

標普 500指數 7,201.09 −0.50%
那斯達克綜合指數 23,733.54 −1.48%
費城半導體指數 8,832.50 −7.88%
資料來源：Bloomberg、元大銀行整理
2. AI相關個股跌幅居前，防禦型類股相對抗跌：個股方面，輝達（NVDA）繼 11月 23
日大漲 31.5%後，11月 24日回跌 5.9%；AI雲端運算服務商 CoreWeave重挫 11.8%，
電力設備商 GE Vernova下跌 4.8%，甫上市的 AI模型開發商 Anthropic亦下跌 6.5%。
相較之下，醫療保健、必需消費等防禦型類股表現較佳，反映市場賣壓仍主要集中於
AI相關投資領域。
二、下跌原因分析
1. 黃仁勳回歸僅解除部分經營疑慮，科技股反彈未能延續：輝達 11月 23日宣布黃仁勳
重返執行長職位，一度大幅提振市場信心，當日輝達股價飆漲 31.5%，標普 500及那
斯達克指數分別大漲約 4.7%及 6.5%。然而，美國兩黨國會議員對黃仁勳回歸仍有批評
聲浪，並主張進一步限制中國取得輝達先進 AI晶片。黃仁勳回歸雖有助於維持輝達產

品開發與長期經營策略的延續性，但公司治理、國家安全及晶片出口管制等問題尚未完
全落幕，前一交易日因事件利多所帶動的買盤缺乏持續性。
2. 美國可能對 16個貿易夥伴加徵關稅，政策不確定性再起：據 Reuters 11/24報導，川
普政府可能針對 16個貿易夥伴推出新一輪關稅措施，涉及中國及歐盟等主要經濟體，
相關消息使市場重新評估全球貿易環境及通膨風險。由於半導體產業高度仰賴跨國供應
鏈，若關稅措施進一步擴大，恐增加伺服器、晶片及其他電子零組件的生產成本，並影
響企業資本支出規劃。此外，關稅亦可能增加聯準會抑制通膨的難度，對科技股評價形
成額外壓力。惟相關措施尚未正式公布，實際影響仍須視課徵範圍及稅率而定。

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. AI投資報酬疑慮與升息壓力未解，高評價族群首當其衝：市場對 AI產業的疑慮，主要
集中於龐大資本支出能否創造相應的投資報酬，加上市場對資料中心監管的疑慮，AI
基礎建設的擴張速度仍存在不確定性。另一方面，市場對聯準會 12月升息已有高度預
期，且關注 2027年是否仍將持續升息，在 11月 25日 PCE物價指數公布前，高評價
及高資本支出的 AI相關企業仍承受評價壓力，賣壓集中於 AI雲端服務、電力設備及新
上市 AI企業等族群。
4. 多重變數未除，短線波動偏高：除了輝達本身的議題，美股後續走勢仍面臨兩大變數，

一是聯準會的升息步調，二是 AI資料中心的興建進度可能受阻。在升息方面，如果聯
準會 12月的升息幅度或未來的升息路徑超出市場預期，科技股評價恐再度受到壓抑。
在資料中心方面，美國部分地方政府陸續限制資料中心興建，可能延後雲端大廠 AI投
資計畫的落實時程，市場對 AI投資前景的疑慮短期內恐難消除。在這兩項變數明朗之
前，美股短期波動預料仍偏高，科技股各次產業的表現也可能進一步分化。因此，建議
投資人分批佈局，避免在市場反彈時過度追價，並保留部分資金，以因應後續市場波動。
圖一:近期主要市場風險
三、市場看法
1. 費半劇烈震盪反映市場信心脆弱，惟並非全然回吐前日漲幅：費城半導體指數 11月 20
日重挫 10.73%，11月 23日旋即大漲 14.03%，11月 24日再度重挫約 7%至 8%，顯
示市場仍處於高度不穩定的階段。然而，若以 Reuters所報導的 11月 24日約 7.9%跌
幅計算，費半指數近兩個交易日累計仍上漲約 5.0%；輝達同期累計漲幅亦約達 23.7%，
顯示黃仁勳回歸所帶來的正面影響並未完全消失。我們認為，11月 24日的下跌部分反

映前一交易日漲幅過大後的獲利了結，以及投資人重新評估尚未解除的風險，而非全然
出現新的產業基本面利空。
2. 輝達治理與政策風險仍是科技股最大變數，短線股價恐持續劇烈波動：黃仁勳回任後首
場全員工大會為違反公司行為準則致歉，並表示輝達不需要傳統接班計畫，反而加深外
界對公司治理的疑慮。此外，美國參議院將於 12月 3日就晶片出口管制舉行聽證會，
黃仁勳將出席；參議院民主黨領袖舒默亦表示將調查政府及輝達高層在復職決定中的角
色，加上路透報導 SEC 正調查其會計作業，相關議題短期內恐持續牽動股價。輝達基

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
本面仍受 AI算力需求支撐，但在治理、出口管制及監管調查明朗前，其股價及對費半
與台灣供應鏈的影響恐維持高波動，投資人宜避免過度集中單一個股。
3. AI算力需求尚未反轉，惟資料中心建設遞延恐影響營收認列時程：輝達 11月 17日公
布的財報仍為 AI需求提供正面佐證：第三季營收達 1,158.4億美元，年成長逾一倍，
優於市場預期的 1,091.1 億美元；第四季營收財測 1,300 億美元（上下 3%），亦高於
預期的 1,252.5億美元，且未納入中國資料中心晶片銷售。股價下跌主要反映市場對需
求成長速度及可持續性的疑慮，而非營運已明顯衰退。另一方面，資料中心監管疑慮主

要影響 AI基礎建設擴張速度，而非長期導入需求，企業仍可透過轉移建設地點、提升
運算效率或租用其他雲端算力因應，惟建設遞延仍可能影響半導體企業出貨及營收認列
時程。我們對 AI產業長期發展仍持正向看法，但不宜低估未來數季財測下修風險，須
關注主要 CSP是否削減資本支出、取消訂單或遞延設備採購。
4. 科技股評價修正有助改善中期風險報酬，投資邏輯轉向個別企業獲利品質的篩選：根據
Reuters 11月 9日報導，標普 500科技類股本益比已一度低於整體標普 500指數，評
價溢價明顯收斂。若企業獲利預估未同步大幅下修，股價修正將有助改善中長期風險報
酬，惟評價降低不代表已經落底，市場仍面臨公司治理、政策、關稅、利率及 AI投資
回收等多重不確定因素。此外，AI 產業鏈各環節的獲利能力及資金需求差異明顯，具
備技術門檻、定價能力及穩健現金流的企業，與高度仰賴外部融資、獲利模式未成熟者，
股價表現將更趨分化。後續需觀察獲利預估是否止穩、股價對利空反應是否鈍化，以及
反彈能否延續，才能確認信心修復。

5. Anthropic上市後表現疲弱，反映市場對 AI商業化的要求提高：Anthropic於 11月
19日掛牌後，股價未能延續初期表現，11月 24日再跌 6.5%。AI模型開發商為算力
需求的重要來源，若資本市場對其評價及融資轉趨審慎，長期恐影響算力擴張速度。不
過，股價疲弱亦可能反映 IPO 定價偏高、獲利不確定性及風險偏好降低，不能直接推
論 AI終端需求下降。後續應更重視 AI企業的營收成長、客戶採用程度、推論成本及現
金消耗速度；若模型使用量持續增加且單位運算成本下降，仍有利長期算力需求。
6. 美股產業輪動持續，科技股重挫尚未演變為全面性基本面惡化：11月 24日費半大跌，
道瓊卻逆勢上漲 0.85%，兩者相差逾 8 個百分點，反映資金仍留在股市，只是由高評
價的 AI類股轉向其他產業。根據 Associated Press報導，標普 500企業有望連續第三
季繳出逾 25%的獲利成長，美國消費者信心亦升至近兩年高點，尚不宜將科技股修正
定調為經濟衰退或多頭格局終結。若非科技產業獲利穩健，資金輪動反有助降低美股對

少數大型科技股的依賴。
7. 12月 FOMC升息與否並非唯一關鍵，後續政策路徑更為重要：由於市場對 12月升息
已有高度預期，若聯準會如預期升息 1碼，其對股市的額外衝擊可能相對有限。更重要
的是，聯準會是否透過會後聲明、最新經濟預測及主席記者會，暗示 2027年仍將持續
升息。若 12月升息後，聯準會轉而強調觀察累積緊縮效果，科技股評價壓力可望逐步
緩和；反之，若通膨預測上修，且官員暗示明年仍有多次升息的必要，則高評價及高資
本支出企業仍可能承壓。因此，11月 25日公布的 PCE物價指數及 12月 FOMC的政
策指引，將是研判科技股後續走勢的重要觀察指標。

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
四、市場展望與投資建議
1. 短線審慎，中期審慎偏正向，關注企業獲利與資金面變化：本波全球科技股修正已由
初期的評價調整，進一步擴大至企業治理、政策風險及資金面的全面性重新定價。尤
其費半指數單日重挫超過 10%，顯示市場風險偏好快速下降，短期賣壓尚未完全解除
。然而，目前尚無充分證據顯示 AI運算需求已出現全面性反轉，輝達最新財報及主要
CSP持續投入 AI基礎建設，仍可為產業中長期成長提供一定支撐。因此，我們對美國
科技股及具備 AI供應鏈優勢的台灣、日本市場，短期維持審慎，中期則採取審慎偏正
向的觀點。不過，科技股未來表現將更依賴實際獲利、訂單能見度及自由現金流，而

非單純的 AI題材。後續應持續關注輝達 SEC調查、CSP資本支出、Fed政策及信用
風險是否擴散；若相關疑慮逐步釐清、企業獲利預估保持穩定，股市可望逐漸回歸基
本面主導。策略上，對中長期仍看好科技產業的投資人，可透過以分批布局及分散類
股配置方式參與，在科技類股部分，應優先考量具備技術競爭力、穩定現金流與較佳
財務體質的企業，避免過度集中於高度依賴外部融資的 AI業者。
2. 美債避險功能重新受到重視，中天期風險報酬相對均衡：Fed升息預期仍使債券市場
面臨利率波動，但隨著全球股市風險偏好下降，美國公債的避險需求已明顯升高。11
月 20日美股重挫，10年期美債殖利率大幅下降，顯示部分資金轉向相對安全的固定
收益資產。儘管 Fed政策尚未明朗，且通膨仍可能造成殖利率反覆波動，但目前美國
公債殖利率仍具有一定吸引力與防禦價值。從資產類別角度來看，我們對中天期美債
維持相對正向看法，主要考量其殖利率水準及利率敏感度的風險報酬較為均衡；超長
天期公債則仍須面對財政赤字及期限溢價風險。投資級債方面，雖然市場對 AI融資風

險的疑慮升高，但整體信用品質仍相對穩健，中期展望維持偏正向，惟須留意信用利
差是否進一步擴大。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%，顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域。數據公布後，市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫。聯準會理事鮑曼表示，決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果；舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示，不宜對升息過於保守，並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持 2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示，在能源價格波動下，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決，歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶；週二台股重挫近千點，外資大幅賣超台股 1653 億

撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對 AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣

市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以 USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡，顯示 USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為 USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈，最高來到 1.1269，終場收在 1.1239，週線上漲 42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆，雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.58%，低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下，週三 2 年期公債得標利率為 1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣，投標倍數回升至 2.86 倍，銀行業得標比重上升至 88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩，惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下，買盤積極追價的意願不強，預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

### Page 3

WinWay Technology (6515.TW)
26 November 2026 Citi Research
3
WinWay Technology
Valuation
We value WinWay using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and likely expansion of its addressable
market. Our target price of NT$6,500 is derived from applying c.30x to 2H27E-1H28E EPS, in line with its historical average PE
since 2023, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the high-end AI GPU testing supply chain,

rising contribution from coaxial socket and SLT solutions, and the increasing value of test content per module. With these
structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a consumer
electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the WinWay stock High Risk based on its historical volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price and our earnings forecasts include: 1) client and

sector concentration – specifically any delays in US AI GPU product roadmap could impact the business outlook for WinWay; 2)
intensifying competition in coaxial and hypersockets from global peers; 3) faster-than-expected testing algorithm
optimization, which could shorten testing durations and reduce the anticipated hardware demand multiplier; and 4) delay of
MEMS Phase 2 mass production.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

## Document 186: 6415矽力杰 宏遠

Source PDF: all report pdf/6415矽力杰 宏遠.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關
條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保證。非經本公司同意，不得
將本報告加以引用或轉載。 
1 
6415 矽力杰( Silergy Corp.)

報告日期：2026/11/16          研究員：王蕎程(Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要: 
 本業獲利持續改善：3Q26 營業利益季增 29.7%，營益率提升
至 13.26%；惟 2Q26 受金融資產評價利益挹注，墊高比較基
期，第三季獲利下滑主要來自業外收益回落，並非本業衰退。 
 AIDC 與車用雙引擎帶動成長：AIDC 營收年增逾 120%，新一
代 SPS 預計 2027 年開始放量；車用受惠 MCU 及高壓大電流
產品導入，帶動單車價值提升，預期 2027 年兩大業務營收均
有望成長逾 50%。 
 製程升級與供貨優勢支撐成長：Gen4 產品持續放量，預期 2027
年底營收占比達 50%，搭 配 12 吋產能布局及 Gen5 新品量產，
有利爭取大型客戶，惟封測產能吃緊及成本上升仍為毛利改善
的觀察重點。

一、3Q26 本業獲利持續改善，預估 4Q26 EPS 2.5 元 
矽力*-KY 為電源管理 IC 設計廠，3Q26 營收 72.59 億元（QoQ 
+20.9%、YoY +52.4%），主要受惠 AIDC、工業及車用需求成長，
以及消費電子客戶提前拉貨。毛利率 47.40%，QoQ +0.4 個百分點，
主要因產品組合改善；營業利益 9.63 億元（QoQ +29.7%），營業
利益率 13.26%，QoQ +0.88 個百分點，本業獲利持續改善。惟 2Q26
受金融資產評價利益 9.59 億元挹注，墊高獲利基期，3Q26 因業外
損益回落至 1.11 億元，稅後淨利降至 9.09 億元（QoQ -42.1%），
EPS 2.34 元，獲利下滑主要來自業外收益減少，並非本業衰退。
展望 4Q26，消費電子需求受第三季提前拉貨及終端市場疲弱影
響，手機、電視等產品出貨轉弱，惟資料中心需求仍維持成長，
預估營收 74.63 億元，毛利率 47.80%，稅後 EPS 2.50 元。

二、產品組合優化，AIDC 與車用為明年主要成長動能 
AIDC 為公司主要成長來源，3Q26 營收占比達 17%，YoY +120%。
隨 AI 伺服器 CPU 及 GPU 功耗提高，主板供電系統需要處理更大
電流，同時降低電力損耗及散熱負擔，帶動高效率電源管理 IC 需
求。其中，新一代 SPS（Smart Power Stage）將功率元件、驅動器
及感測功能整合於單一封裝，可改善高電流供電效率並縮小電路
板空間，目前已進入多家客戶驗證階段，預期 2027 年開始貢獻營
收。公司表示，北美客戶對相關產品的需求較為迫切，預期放量
速度將快於中國客戶；此外，伺服器主板、資料中心通訊設備及
SSD 為 AIDC 主要成長應用，三者合計可占 AI 相關營收約 70%～

三、車用單車價值提升，光通訊與 12 吋製程布局 
車用方面，公司持續增加單一車款的晶片供應種類，除既有電源
管理產品外，高壓、大電流電源 IC、MCU 及電池管理相關產品陸
續導入域控制器、電驅等應用，帶動單車產品價值提升。公司表
示，高階車款單車產品價值預計由 2026 年底的 200～220 美元，提
升至 2027 年的 270～300 美元；海外新客戶近期已開始小批量出
貨，明年貢獻可望增加，惟中國客戶仍為主要營收來源，預期 2027
年車用營收成長逾 50%。光通訊方面，隨 AI 資料中心傳輸速率提
升，光模組對供電效率、功耗及散熱的要求同步提高，帶動高效
率電源轉換、雷射驅動電源及整合式電源管理產品需求。公司已
布局 NPO 及 CPO 相關產品，並進入客戶設計導入階段。

製程方面，公司布局 12 吋晶圓產能，目前的產能可支應至少 20
億美元營收規模。隨 AIDC 及車用需求增加，類比 IC 供應鏈產能
趨緊，部分同業仍以 8 吋製程為主，矽力則因 12 吋產能準備較為
充足，有利爭取重視供貨穩定性的大型客戶。公司表示，Gen4 產
品持續放量，預期 2027 年底營收占比約 50%，Gen5 則預計於 2027
年量產。新製程除改善成本結構外，也能支援 AIDC 對電源轉換效
率、功率密度及產品整合度的要求，有利提升高階產品比重。

供應鏈方面，目前主要瓶頸在封裝測試，受設備供應不足及裝機
速度限制，產品生產週期由原先約 4 個月延長至 9 個月，公司須
提前備妥晶圓等待後段排產，帶動在製品增加。預期 2027 年上半
年封測產能仍難明顯改善，較大幅度的產能紓解可能落在 2027 年
下半年至 2028 年。雖然公司前段晶圓供應相對充足，但後段產能
限制仍可能影響出貨速度，且成本上升未必能完全轉嫁客戶。

四、預估 2026 / 2027 年稅後 EPS 10.87 / 14.72 元 
預估 2026 年營收 255.85 億元（YoY +36.0%），毛利率 47.58%，營
業利益 31.33 億元，稅後 EPS 10.87 元。展望 2027 年，受惠 AIDC
及車用新品放量、Gen4 良率改善與高階產品比重提升，預估營收
319.81 億元（YoY +25.0%），毛利率提升至 50.07%；營業利益 57.83
億元（YoY +84.6%）。考量業外收益回歸正常水準，預估稅後 EPS 
14.72 元（YoY +35.4%）。 
五、投資建議「買進」，目標價 600 元 
綜合考量：(1) AIDC 需求持續成長，帶動伺服器主板、SSD 及光

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
矽力杰股份有限公司（簡稱：矽力 KY；代號：6415）主要從事類
比、數位混和 IC 之研發設計及銷售，產品應用範圍廣泛，主要
應用於 3C 產業、雲端伺服器、工業類、汽車、5G 通訊等相關
領域，包括 LED 照明、平板電腦、筆記型電腦、固態硬碟、視
頻監控、伺服器、數位機上盒、智能 音箱、智慧型手機、多媒體
電視、LED 背光模組、路由器、行動電源、汽車電子、通訊基地
台及智能電錶等，並持續增加各種終端應用，如 TV Panel、手機
快充、醫療領域等。

矽力 KY 目前產品主要是類比 IC 晶片，產品廣泛應用於消費性
電子產品、工業用產品、資訊產品、5G 網路通訊產品及汽車電子
產品。針對終端產品設計需求，該公司可提供不同的晶片，例如
平板電腦、電池充電管理晶片、直流對直流轉換晶片、過流保護
晶片、靜電防護裝置、LED 背光驅動晶片以及 PMU 等；針對 LED 
照明，該公司可提供 LED 照明驅動晶片；針對機上盒，該公司
可提供交流對直流轉換晶片，直流對直流轉換晶片，過流保護晶
片，EMI 防護裝置等；智慧電表，則提供包含能源監控管理(Energy 
measurement IC)的系統晶片 System on Chip (SOC) 以及高精度電
流檢測方案。

二. 產業趨勢概況 
市場概況 
(1)半導體市場概況與 IC 設計業 
半導體產業為工業類設備、資訊電腦、網路通訊、消費性及汽車
電子產品應用的關鍵性產業，亦是目前主導性的基礎產業之一，
隨著 3C(電腦 Computer、通訊 Communication、消費 Consumer)產
品的蓬勃發展和工業類產品智慧化和低能耗的需求，產品生命週
期縮短而更加快速，並朝多元化及豐富化發展，隨著產品推陳出
新與成長，均促進未來半導體需求及市場穩定發展。國內半導體
產業鏈發展日益成熟，有完整的上、中、下游分工體系，使得 IC 
設計公司得以專精於從事設計工作，再將 IC 的製造委託給晶圓
代工製作成晶圓半成品，經過前段測試後再交由封裝廠進行切割
及封裝，最後再經專業測試廠做後段測 試後，完成最後產品，也
造就了良好的 IC 設計產業發展環境。 IC 元件一般可分為數位
邏輯 IC 及類比 IC 兩大類。數位 IC 利用 0、1 非連續性方式
傳遞訊號，多用於處理數位資訊如計算、控制、儲存等，強調尺
寸、速度與功耗(Process Driven)。類比 IC 則負責處理來自自然界
的連續性類比訊號，如光、 熱、電、速度、壓力等物理量，並作
為橋接上述各類物理訊息與數位電子系統的媒介，需同時藉由製
程、電路設計和半導體元件物理的相互配合，在晶片效能及成本 
上尋求最適化，由於其決定了數位產品所能呈現的最終呈現品

質，因此更為注重元件的特性(Design Driven)，琢磨於可靠度、穩
定度、功率消耗、能源轉換效率、電壓電流控制能力等

(2)電源管理 IC 
隨著環保與節能議題發酵，節能科技為目前重要發展課題之一，
而類比 IC 中 的電源管理 IC 正扮演著不可或缺的關鍵性角
色，電源管理 IC 主要作為在不同的系統運作狀態下，隨時控制
並保持系統內適當電流與電壓正常供給。而電源供應器的好壞，
將會直接影響電子產品的使用壽命。近年來電源供應器均朝向輕
薄短小、省電、耐用的方向發展。而要產品達到這些目標的重要
關鍵，乃在於如何把電源供應器做到高效率(高效能)、高穩壓率和
低雜訊干擾，這其中所有參與管制功能的 IC 即被稱為「電源控
制、管理類比 IC」，每年市場均保持穩定成長，消費電子、5G 網
路通信、移動互聯領域、雲端伺服器、工業應用都是目前最主要
市場，未來並朝向人工智慧及電動車及車連網方向發展。電源管
理單元(PMU)是一種高度集成的電源管理方案，將傳統分立的若干
類電源管理器件整合在單個的封裝之內，可實現更高的電源轉換
效率、更低功耗，以及更少的元件數。PMU 在有限的體積內集成
了盡可能多的電源通道，根據各個子單元的需求、提供高效率的
供電，或提供低雜訊的乾淨電源，又或者是系統必須的上電時序
控制，成為為系統供電的首選。過去十年智慧型手持式裝置在市
場快速發展，總體來看，終端市場整體還將保持快速成長，在此
大背景下，PMU 的市場需求有望實現較之前市場預期更高的發展
態勢。隨著智慧型手持式產品的大螢幕化、輕薄化，電池的體積
也將會越來越小，在相應的電池容量不變的情況下，要求整機的
續航能力要更強，除了不斷降低晶片功耗改善以外，對電源的智
慧化管理也變得不可或缺，因此未來 PMU 市場還會有不錯發展。

未來展望 
電源管理 IC 是類比 IC 最基礎和重要的產品，近年來電源管理
一直是半導體領域的熱門市場之一，且電源管理 IC 是所有電子
產品必備的關鍵零組件，屬於高功率類比電路技術，主要用途為
提供電子產品穩定且適當電壓的電源，將直接影響電子產品的使
用壽命。隨著全球 3C 產品的功能不斷增加，汽車自動駕駛和工
業自動 化需求，終端應用漸漸走向低耗電、輕薄短小與多功能整
合以及對產品壽命與可靠度要求更高的趨勢，為達到此目的，電
源管理 IC 必須做到使電源系統效率提升、節能及縮小 IC 元件
封裝以減少所佔用之電路板面積等重點。另隨著 CPU 不斷向上 
躍進，對電源的穩定性及電壓的精準度也就更為要求。此外，高
整合度不但能減少零件數量，達到降低系統耗電以及提升系統可
靠度及品質等效益，並可提高生產良率，且降低生產工時，進而

源將成為電源管理 IC 設計的重要方向。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一、預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.50 元 
矽力*-KY 3Q26 營收 72.59 億元（QoQ +20.9%、YoY +52.4%），受
惠 AIDC、工業及車用需求成長，毛利率 47.40%，營業利益 9.63
億元（QoQ +29.7%）；惟 2Q26 金融資產評價利益墊高比較基期，
3Q26 業外收益回落，稅後淨利 9.09 億元（QoQ -42.1%），EPS 2.34
元。展望 4Q26，消費電子需求轉弱，惟資料中心需求維持成長，
預估營收 74.63 億元（QoQ +2.8%），毛利率 47.80%，稅後淨利 9.76
億元，EPS 2.50 元。

二、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.87 元 
預估 2026 年營收 255.85 億元（YoY +36.0%），受惠 AIDC 及車用
需求增加，毛利率 47.58%，營業毛利 121.74 億元；營業利益 31.33
億元，營業利益率 12.25%，業外損益 15.70 億元，稅後淨利 42.24
億元，稅後 EPS 10.87 元。

三、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 14.72 元 
展望 2027 年，受惠 AIDC 新一代 SPS 產品導入、車用單車價值提
升，以及 Gen4 製程良率改善與高階產品比重增加，預估全年營收
319.81 億元（YoY +25.0%），營業毛利 160.12 億元，毛利率 50.07%；
營業利益 57.83 億元（YoY +84.6%），營業利益率 18.08%，業外
損益 4.51 億元，稅後淨利 57.34 億元（YoY +35.7%），稅後 EPS 14.72
元。

### Page 1

請參閱末頁之聲明

2026.11.23 
梁會鑫 C00255@ibfs.com.tw 
7722.TT LINEPAY 
 累計營收再度創高，惟匯損衝擊獲利增幅收窄

[調升評價]   
   公司簡介 
公司於 2015 年設立，為國內第三方支付龍頭，丁雄注為董事長兼總經理，分別有台
北富邦銀行、聯邦銀行入股，並持有兩間子公司，於 2025 年 7 月經金管會核准設立
電子支付子公司後，已於 2025 年 12 月啟用電子支付。   
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 303 
【前次投資建議】區間操作，303 
【大盤指數/股價】36033 / 225

➢ 前 9 月營收雙位數增長，惟毛利率因營業費用轉列營業成本縮減 
LinePay 累計前 9 月營收為 64.33 億元，YoY+15%，而 LinePay 前 9 月
毛利率為 24.9%，YoY-8.6ppts.，主因電子支付上線後，原本列為營業
費用的管理與研發支出，部分轉列至營業成本所致，惟毛利率亦較 1H26
有所提升，反映電子支付營收缺口逐漸補上，而營業淨利率仍受成本及
費用增長而降至 6.8%，YoY-1.9ppts.，且 3Q26 稅後淨利受業外匯兌損
失影響下較 3Q25 YoY-22.6%下，令整體 2026 年前 9 月稅後淨利僅增
至 4.35 億元，YoY+5.7%， EPS 為 6.12 元。 
➢ 2026 年營收預估將因短期營收出現缺口使年增低於過往水準 
綜觀 4Q26，LinePay 10 月營收為 7.33 億元，YoY+4.1%，占 4Q26 達
成率為 31.6%，營收年增低於過去水準反映短期營收出現缺口所致，考
量前 9 月核心手續費年增維持逾 20%，此外 4Q26 亦有百貨週年慶、電
商節慶與旅遊旺季可望為支付交易量帶來季節性動能，綜合考量之下，
投研部預估 4Q26 營收為 23.17 億元，QoQ+5%，YoY+6.4%，稅後淨
利則為 1.56 億元，QoQ+11.8%，YoY+63.3%，EPS 2.29 元。 
➢ 電支業務有望加速拓展，調升至買進評等 
展望 2026 年，考量 LinePay 與眾多銀行推出多檔活動，預期將可挹注
全年交易量提升，並帶動營收成長，並參考 LinePay 開始施行電子支付
業務，因而投研部預估 2026 年營收為 87.5 億元，YoY+11.2%，此外，
因電子支付業務毛利率較現行手續費毛利率高，預估隨電子支付業務 4
月轉虧為盈後，將有望帶動毛利率逐季改善，因而預估將使毛利率較
2026 年前 9 月增長至 25.2%，YoY-7.5ppts.，預期亦能使淨利有所增
長，稅後淨利則為 5.91 億元，YoY+16.5%，EPS 為 8.58 元。 
綜合前述，以 11/20 收盤價 225 元估算目前評價為 PER 26X，考量過往
本益比區間為 25X~100X，目前評價處於低緣，主要反映資本市場下行，
拖累民眾消費動能縮減所致，考量 2027 年營收仍將持續創高並維持雙
位數增長，並參考電子支付業務用戶擴張至 450 萬人，有望加速帶動電
子支付業務增長，毛利率外溢效果亦將於 2027 年逐步顯現，再加上韓
國、日本等海外市場深化跨境支付，進一步提升 LinePay 獲利能力，因
而投研部調升至買進投資評等，目標價 303 元(2027F PE 30X)。

7722 LINEPAY 加權指數

### Page 2

- 2 -

個股報告

拓展海外市場提升用戶數量及支付據點，公司目標 2026 年交易量突破 1 兆元 
2025 年財務分析中，營收主要來自於手續費收入佔 82%，而金融推廣服務亦佔約 14%，
全年毛利率則為 32.7%，若拆分為細項的話，作為營收主體的手續費收入毛利率約為
20%，金融推廣服務則有 93%。而截至 4Q25，身為國內第三方支付龍頭的 LinePay
用戶數已超過 1,360 萬人、平均每 2 人就有 1 人使用，而用戶綁卡數亦超過 2,100 萬
張，佔全體流通信用卡數逾 1/3，全台支付據點也已成長至 66 萬間，韓國支付據點亦
逾 8 萬間，全球支付據點加總超過 74 萬間，2025 年全年 LinePay 交易額亦達 8,680
億元。 
圖 1：2025 年 LinePay 營收收入及佔比

資料來源：LinePay 
穩定幣逐漸成全球趨勢、台灣政府若開放，LinePay 可望受惠 
隨美國天才法案通過，2025 年全球掀起穩定幣浪潮，美國、香港、新加坡、日本及英
國相繼提升其國內使用穩定幣比例，而穩定幣主要用途有兩項，其一為跨境支付，通過
以穩定幣跨境支付，相較於過往經由銀行結算系統進行跨境支付時，將可節省大量的
手續費成本以及交易時間，因此其為目前主要使用用途，而第二項用途則是提供避險
手段，在阿根廷及其餘新興國家中，其本國貨幣通膨較為嚴重，民眾普遍不信任政府發
行之法幣，而穩定幣則在其中提供穩定的美元避險功能，因此穩定幣亦於新興國家中
大幅使用。而考量 LinePay 具有足夠科技實力建立穩定幣平台並發行之，因而雖台灣
監管力道趨嚴，但台灣政府若開放，預期 LinePay 可望受惠而持續具成長空間。

圖 3：各國穩定幣流通量百分比

資料來源：Artemis 
2026 年前 9 月營收維持雙位數增長，惟毛利率因營業費用轉列營業成本而縮減 
LinePay 2026 年累計前 9 月營收為 64.33 億元，YoY+15%，而 LinePay 前 9 月毛利
率為 24.9%，YoY-8.6ppts.，主因電子支付上線後，原本列為營業費用的管理與研發支
出，部分轉列至營業成本所致，惟毛利率亦較 1H26 有所提升，反映電子支付營收缺
口逐漸補上，此外由於 LinePay 2025 年申請電支執照及持續投入 IDC，使其研發費用
持續維持高點，以及行銷及管理費用亦有所增長，預期在電支平台建置完成及部分營
業費用轉列至營業成本後，整體營業費用率下降至 18%，YoY-6.8ppts.，惟營業淨利
率仍受成本及費用增長而降至 6.8%，YoY-1.9ppts.，且 3Q26 稅後淨利受業外匯兌損
失影響下較 3Q25 YoY-22.6%下，令整體 2026 年前 9 月稅後淨利僅增至 4.35 億元，
YoY+5.7%，EPS 為 6.12 元。 
2026 年營收預估將因短期營收出現缺口使年增低於過往水準 
綜觀 4Q26，LinePay 10 月營收為 7.33 億元，YoY+4.1%，占 4Q26 達成率為 31.6%，
約略符合投研部預期，營收年增低於過去水準主要反映 2025 年 12 月起轉入自營電子
支付，結束部分合作合約，短期營收出現缺口所致，考量前 9 月核心手續費年增維持
逾 20%，再加上電子支付業務已於 4 月轉虧為盈，此外 4Q26 亦有百貨週年慶、電商
節慶與旅遊旺季可望為支付交易量帶來季節性動能，因而投研部預估其營收成長幅度
雖將較 2025 年收窄，惟 4Q26 營收及獲利有望改善，綜合考量之下，投研部預估 4Q26
營收為 23.17 億元，按季節成長率來看 QoQ+5%，YoY+6.4%，稅後淨利則為 1.56 億
元，QoQ+11.8%，YoY+63.3%，EPS 2.29 元。 
電支業務有望加速拓展，調升至買進評等，目標價 303 元 
展望 2026 年，考量公司與眾多銀行推出多檔活動，預期將可挹注全年交易量提升，並
帶動營收成長，並參考 LinePay 開始施行電子支付業務，向店家收取之手續費收入不
必再與 iPASS MONEY 拆分收益，預期亦將挹注營收，惟參考 2025 年 12 月起轉入自
營電子支付，結束部分合作合約，短期營收出現缺口，所幸隨乘車碼、好友推薦、未成
年帳戶等多元場景擴大用戶規模，帶動電子支付用戶擴張至 450 萬人，並有望加速帶
動電子支付業務增長，綜合考量下，投研部預估 2026 年營收為 87.5 億元，YoY+11.2%，
此外，因電子支付業務毛利率較現行手續費毛利率高，預估隨電子支付業務 4 月轉虧
為盈後，將有望帶動毛利率逐季改善，因而預估將使毛利率較 2026 年前 9 月增長至
25.2%，YoY-7.5ppts.，且 LinePay 與眾多銀行皆有合作關係，在推出電子錢包服務
後，亦可與部分不願意使用信用卡之店家進行不同模式的合作，而隨著電支業務上線，

### Page 4

- 4 -

個股報告 
預期研發費用將有所縮減，再加上電子支付部分，銀行將負擔大部分行銷費用，綜合考
量下也將節省部分營業費用，預期亦能使淨利有所增長，稅後淨利則為 5.91 億元，
YoY+16.5%，EPS 為 8.58 元。 
2027 年部分，則預期在電子支付業務成長穩定，再加上海外獲利增長而使整體營收及
稅後淨利再度增長，投研部預估 LinePay 2027 年營收為 103.54 億元，YoY+18.3%，
稅後淨利則為 6.86 億元，YoY+16%，EPS 10.09 元。 
綜合前述，以 11/20 收盤價 225 元估算目前評價約為 PER 26X，考量 LinePay 過往
本益比區間為 25X~100X，目前評價處於低緣，主要反映資本市場下行，拖累民眾消
費動能縮減所致，考量 LinePay 2027 年營收仍將持續創高並維持雙位數增長，且非
現金支付仍有成長空間，再加上電子支付業務挹注後，整體成長潛力仍具前景，並考
量 Line pay money 電子支付公司運用乘車碼、好友推薦、未成年帳戶等多元活動擴
大用戶規模，帶動電子支付用戶擴張至 450 萬人，並有望加速帶動電子支付業務增
長，毛利率提升之外溢效果亦將於 2027 年逐步顯現，再加上韓國、日本等海外市場
亦透過高額回饋與點數活動深化跨境支付，進一步提升 LinePay 獲利能力，因而投研
部調升至買進投資評等，目標價 303 元(2027F PE 30X)。

## Document 198: 7799禾榮科 永豐

Source PDF: all report pdf/7799禾榮科 永豐.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/11/23

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

禾榮科 (7799 TT) 
獲利成長列車即將啟航

永豐觀點 
台灣藥證年底取證在即，1H27 起設備陸續商轉，營收進入兌現期，隨全球佈局深
化與自製設備落地，禾榮科技術、平台、生態綜效全面釋放，獲利加速成長期將至。 
 投資評價與建議 
建議買進：禾榮科自 2027 年起將迎來營運轉捩點，正式從臨床驗證走向實質營收
兌現。隨著適應症拓展、區域市場放量、複合式療法推進至一線治療，禾榮科的全
球商業模式將加速複製，在設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利
將迎來跳躍式增長，建議買進，目標價以 NPV 估算(WACC=6%，永續性獲利成長
10%)為 825 元。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：禾榮科技成立於 2017 年，為一專注加速器型硼中子捕獲治療系統（AB-
BNCT）技術開發與商業化廠商，並以成為 BNCT 領域值得信賴之全球品牌，提供
癌症精準治療，成為 BNCT 全方位解決方案提供者為目標。公司擁有完整研發、製
造與經營管理團隊，成員具多元專業背景，主要股東為泛漢民集團。在資金、營運
到研發管理團隊陣容堅強下，實力強大有利公司在高資本癌症高階醫療設備治療市
場之拓展。 
台灣藥證年底取證在即，1H27 營收正式入帳，獲利加速成長：禾榮科 BNCT 設備
已於 2024 年 11 月取證，硼中子捕獲治療用藥 BPA 藥證申請中，研究部預計今年
底前將取證，1H27 起，隨北榮設備商轉並進行病患收受，南京明碁醫院設備依完
工比例認列營收，及中國醫持續進行相關適應症之臨床試驗與正式治療等多項業務
併進下，營收將進入兌現期，預估 2027 年營收可達 29.6 億元，2028 年在南京明
碁醫院設備續依完工比例認列營收，新加坡、竹銘等國內外 BOT 案接棒商轉挹注
下，預估營收將持續跳增至 41.2 億元(+39.3YoY)，獲利成長列車正式開動。 
圖一：禾榮科 2027 年起將進入營收兌現期

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 2

2

技術領先 x 完整平台 x 生態精準，禾榮科構築 BNCT 行業壁壘：硼中子捕獲治療
（BNCT）相較於傳統重粒子治療（每人平均需 10-20 次），僅需 1 次療程即可
完成，具備顯著的臨床優勢。目前全球除日本住友（Sumitomo）設備已於日本商
轉外，禾榮科的 AB-BNCT 系統也於 2024 年順利取得 TFDA 醫療器材認證，高
居全球開發進度第二快之廠商，且相較住友及其餘臨床中競品，禾榮科 AB-BNCT
為高效率超熱中子產生系統，質子轉換效率(Proton Efficiency)達 4.8，為競爭對手
4 倍以上，質子電流與功率最低，運轉成本亦為最低，由於中子產生效率高，治療
室中殘留的輻射劑量低，只需 10 分鐘待核種衰變後即可進行下位病人治療，一天
治療人數可達 6 人以上，使用效率顯著優於 Sumitomo，可支撐多適應症商業化
與治療量規模化外，禾榮科致力提供以加速器型硼中子捕獲治療（AB-BNCT）為
核心的一條龍整體解決方案，公司橫跨「治療前精準評估、治療中高效計畫與定位、
標靶照射治療、治療後長效維護」四大階段，全面構築競爭對手難以跨越的技術與
行業壁壘。 
跨足全球與多元適應症為禾榮科中長期營運創造強大堆疊效應：目前單台 BNCT 設
備年治療容量可達 1,500 人，以台灣 BNCT 潛在市場年新增病患來約 5.5 萬人，
五年累積人數預估達 24 萬人，復發率 30-40%，設備需求量達超過 50 台，禾榮
科自 2024 年醫療器材取證以來，恩慈治療上已累 300 名以上病患，涵蓋近 30 項
腫瘤適應症，並在復發型頭頸癌、三陰性乳癌等多個適應症上展現優越療效，成功
吸引國內外指標性醫院展開深度合作。研究部認為 2026 年底取證後，2027 年起
BNCT 有機會以多重適應症收治病人。 
台灣市場：除已開展治療的中醫大及 1H27 預計商轉的北榮外，新竹竹銘與義大醫
院也預計於 2028 年接棒營運。此外，國內大型指標教學醫院正與公司進行乳癌臨
床試驗，未來可望深化合作範疇。 
海外市場：佈局正全面加速，包括日本合作方已有病患來台接受治療，東南亞市場
以新加坡為核心，除已於今年五月提出醫材註冊申請，公司近日也公告將以 13.8
億元入主新加坡上市公司 SAM Holdings(SGX：9G2)，取得 50.09%控制權，因
SAM 在新加坡擁有先進之質子治療中心與光子放射治療服務，雙方結合後，禾榮
科將導入自家加速器型硼中子捕獲治療（AB-BNCT）系統，打造東南亞首座「雙
粒子癌症治療平台」，預計 2028 年以醫療中心模式啟動治療，提供全方位精準腫
瘤治療服務，因新加坡具備高度國際化與健全的醫療法規體系，是連結東南亞醫療
市場的關鍵門戶，故未來將以新加坡為樞紐，全力搶攻東南亞與國際醫療商機，美
國市場則已與邁阿密、明尼蘇達、紐約三地指標醫院接洽，並於六月向美國 FDA 
圖二：禾榮科在行業中已建立強勁的技術壁壘

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 3

3

提出上市前諮詢申請。 
整體而言，禾榮科將自 2027 年起迎來營運轉捩點，正式從臨床驗證走向實質營收
兌現。隨著適應症拓展、區域市場放量、複合式療法推進至一線治療，禾榮科的全
球商業模式將加速複製，在設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利
將迎來跳躍式增長。 
2028 年達國產國造目標，設備交期有效掌握下，對業務發展將如虎添翼：禾榮科
AB-BNCT 治療系統目前 90%之子系統由禾榮科自主開發，為全面掌控設備交期、
供應鏈管理與確保關鍵材料之供應，禾榮科與 Advanced Cyclotron Systems Inc.
簽署加速器技術授權移轉案，並於 10/16 公告於苗栗購地打造高階醫療加速器研
發製造與藥物製造基地，整合設備製造、系統組裝、測試驗證與藥物開發能力，預
計 2028 年達到「國產國造」目標。研究部認為，相較於競爭者多以單一產品切入
市場，禾榮科具備「AB-BNCT 設備＋硼中子治療藥物＋治療計劃系統」的完整整
合能力，能為醫療院所提供一站式解決方案，形成明顯的差異化優勢。未來隨國產
自製設備問世，在設備建置時程可有效掌控的前提下，將有望進一步加速全球設備
的鋪設進度與國際業務拓展。 
財務預估：禾榮科在營收結構上，藥物部分將依病患數按比例收取治療費，設備則
採賣斷、BOT 與租賃等多元模式有不同認列方式，多管齊下加速全球布局並建立
可複製的癌症診療平台。獲利部分，由於藥物收入與治療收入的毛利率達 70-80%，
設備認列的毛利率約 60-70%，後續隨著藥物與治療比重增加，獲利率可望提升。 
預估禾榮科 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 3.98 億元，稅後 EPS-2.52 元，2027
年在北榮設備可完全認列收入，中醫大及北榮開始進行正式病患治療收費，與南京
明碁醫院設備依完工比例認列營收下，預估營收將跳增至 29.6 億元，稅後淨利 16.1
億元，稅後 EPS 10.2 元；隨合作醫院持續進行設備商轉，預估 2028 年竹銘醫院、
義大醫院與新加坡 NUH 可望開始收治病患，營收預估達 41.2 億元(+39.3YoY)，
稅後淨利 22.1 億元(+39.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 13.94 元；2029 年預估裝機醫院陸續
商轉以及病患持續累積，經常性收入占比擴大，營收 53.9 億元(+30.8%YoY)，稅
後淨利 27.5 億元(+24.8%YoY)，稅後 EPS 17.39 元，後續每年隨著醫院設備裝機
與治療人數累積，獲利可望呈現每年成長。禾榮科的全球商業模式將加速複製，在
設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利將迎來跳躍式增長，建議買
進，目標價以 NPV 估算(WACC=6%，永續性獲利成長 10%)為 825 元。 
圖三：禾榮科全球市場拓展狀況

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

## Document 204: [000270] 기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것배당은 지켜질 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것배당은 지켜질 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 220: [005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(YoY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(YoY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(YoY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(YoY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(YoY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(YoY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

emanating from the Middle East."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the
past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but
also conveyed that "Unless progress towards resolving the
conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk

to inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may
be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and comments made in
the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this
was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

## Document 282: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including 
companies that could benefit from potential tariff rollbacks. 
Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on 
expectations that potential policy changes could provide a 
tailwind for the healthcare sector. 
On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues 
amid concerns around potential data centre moratoriums. 
Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around 
data centre spending, investors should remain selective. 
After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a 
lower recommended allocation compared to September. 
While we believe the mid-term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre-election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre-market trading, although down from intra-day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015-2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12-month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.  
However, despite historical readings supporting a near-term 
DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

likely be modest and short-lived unless compounded by a

Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer 
investment grade corporates at three-to-seven year 
tenors to earn income and manage interest rate risk. The 
US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30-year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more 
Fed rate hikes between now and H1 2027 as still sticky 
inflation and the robust economy support further tightening.  
In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe 
there is a higher near-term risk of a restart of combat 
operations in Iran given the conclusion of the midterm 
elections. Given that, we believe there is a risk that oil 
prices could see a short-term spike, despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

We expect oil prices to drift towards USD 80 a barrel as 
oil supply chains adapt, and Congress restrains the US 
defense budget

## Document 332: Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Macro Outlook 2027: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

We entered 2026 expecting sturdy global growth and slower inflation. The war in Iran 
derailed this inflation forecast, but the global economy proved more resilient than 
feared at the start of the war. 
Even though global headline inflation is on course to end 2026 at 3.7% on a Q4/Q4 
basis—1.3pp higher than our forecast from last year—we estimate that the global 
economy still grew at a remarkably unremarkable 2.7%, in line with potential and only 
0.1pp below our projections from our 2026 outlook (Exhibit 1). Our interpretation is that 
surging AI capex, easier financial conditions, and (in the US, Germany, and parts of Asia) 
fiscal expansion mostly offset the energy supply shock. As a result of these tailwinds, 
GDP growth only modestly underperformed in the US (-0.3pp), Euro area (-0.3pp), and 
China (-0.2pp) while significantly outperforming in Asian economies such as Taiwan 
(+8.6pp) and Korea (+1.9pp) that benefited from surging AI exports.

Growth Remains Solid but Unremarkable in 2027 
We expect another year of solid but unremarkable GDP growth in 2027, but several of 
the impulses that shaped global growth in 2026 will look different. 
The headwind from the energy supply shock will likely flip next year as energy prices 
normalize. Our commodity strategists’ baseline is that continued Middle East supply 
adaptation will lower Brent prices to $78/bbl by end-2027 with a similar normalization 
in global LNG prices (Exhibit 2). Based on these projections, we forecast that the energy 
price impulse to global growth will swing from a 0.4pp drag on global growth in 2026 to 
a modest boost in 2027 (although we see a continued drag in Europe from delayed 
energy cost pass-through to consumers).

Exhibit 1: Inflation Surged, but Growth Proved Resilient in 2026

### Page 3

A positive growth impulse from energy prices should outweigh the effect of tighter 
financial conditions and a smaller fiscal impulse in 2027. Our GS Financial Conditions 
Index (GS FCI) has tightened by around 50bp in the US and by 30bp in developed 
markets (DMs) over the last three months on the back of lower equity prices and higher 
rates. It now implies a moderate drag on growth over the next few quarters (Exhibit 3). 
We do not expect meaningful fiscal consolidation owing to higher rates in 2027, but the 
large fiscal expansions of the last few years are fading. The main exception is Japan, 
where consumption tax cuts, an increase in defense spending, and the Takaichi 
administration’s investment plans point to a meaningful 0.7pp boost to GDP growth. We 
also expect modestly positive impulses in Germany and the US—where the Democratic 
victory in November’s US election suggests some of the spending cuts from the 2025 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act will be reversed—but a continued fiscal drag in the UK that 
leaves the DM fiscal impulse only slightly positive.

### Page 4

The main global growth impulse that we do not expect to change in 2027 is increased AI 
capex. We forecast that nominal global AI investment will rise from $1.1tn in 2026 to 
$1.8tn in 2027, mostly driven by an increase of over $500bn in hardware spending in the 
US (Exhibit 4). We expect the investment surge to be frontloaded, with the 
year-over-year change in investment as a share of GDP peaking in 2027H1 (the level of 
investment as a share of GDP peaks in 2028 under our forecast). Even with the expected 
slowdown, the impact on GDP growth will be sizable: we estimate that AI investment will 
boost GDP growth by 0.4pp globally. And although we again estimate only a small 0.2pp 
boost to measured US GDP growth in 2027 owing to continued undercounting of 
semiconductor investment and chip-related intellectual property exports, we estimate a 
larger 0.6pp boost to “true” GDP growth after correcting for these measurement biases.

### Page 5

The other notable growth impulse from 2026 that will continue in 2027 is a drag on 
rest-of-the-world growth from China. Our forecasts for China remain above consensus 
for 2027 (4.5% GS vs. 4.4% consensus), but this again reflects very robust expectations 
for Chinese exports and a further increase in China’s trade surplus (Exhibit 5). By 
contrast, we expect domestic demand growth to remain weak in 2027 owing to a 1.3pp 
of GDP property sector drag and weak underlying consumer spending, resulting in a 
modest 0.1pp headwind to GDP growth for the rest of the world, with larger growth 
drags in manufacturing-heavy economies such as Germany (-0.3pp) and Central Europe 
(-0.2pp).

### Page 6

Taken together, our 2027 global GDP growth forecast is modestly optimistic at 2.8%, a 
pace that is 0.1pp above our estimate of potential. As has been the case for the last 
several years, we are more optimistic on US growth (2.4% vs. 2.2% consensus) owing to 
resilient investment and more downbeat on the European growth outlook (0.9% vs. 
1.3%) owing to tighter financial conditions and our expectation that higher energy 
prices will start to bite this winter. We also expect continued robust growth in South 
Korea (3.2% vs. 2.7%) and Taiwan (6.3% vs. 5.9%) reflecting another year of strong AI 
exports.

Downside Risks 
We see risks around our baseline global growth forecasts as skewed to the downside for 
three reasons. 
1. A Renewed Energy Shock 
The first and most immediate risk is that an escalation of the war in Iran could lead to 
more significant energy price increases, creating growth headwinds that—unlike in 
2026—FCI and fiscal impulses would not offset in 2027. While our commodity strategists 
see risks around their energy price baselines as two-sided, they put more weight on an 
upside scenario where intensified shipping attacks, damage to oil infrastructure, and a 
more persistent risk premium push oil prices up to $132/bbl (Exhibit 7). If these risks 
materialize, the energy price impulse would remain a moderate drag on global GDP 
growth in 2027.

2. Bond Market Turbulence 
Second, we worry about a bigger fiscal risk premium. In 2026, we saw the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—show 
up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates. Although rates have eased 
somewhat over the last few weeks, the DM yield curve remains 85bp above its level in 
early 2026. If sustained, we estimate that this increase would subtract a cumulative 0.5% 
from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment (Exhibit 
8). Only part of this drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect 
bond yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.

Exhibit 7: A Further Rise in Oil Prices Owing to Middle East Escalation Could Derail the Growth Outlook

3. A Weaker AI Impulse
The transition to an AI-enabled economy is occurring in two phases. The first phase
involves an investment boom that raises both corporate profits and global GDP growth
but features only limited productivity gains and labor market disruption (because it
takes firms several years to adopt AI and reorganize to take full advantage of the new
technology). The second phase involves lower hardware investment but more visible
business reorganization, as well as more substantial productivity gains and labor market

disruption.
To illustrate this transition, we overlay a simulated AI capex path with our estimates of
the labor market impacts under a 10-year adoption timeline (Exhibit 10). The capex
path assumes that US AI investment follows equity analysts’ projections through 2032,
after which the capital stock converges toward a long-run equilibrium level.1  Under our
baseline calibration, the AI capital stock eventually converges to a long-run level of
around 9% of GDP, and 9% of total US workers are displaced. (We continue to expect

most of these displaced workers to find new jobs, including in new occupations that are
created by AI).
Under these assumptions, the transition from the first phase to the second phase
doesn’t occur until 2028-2029, when we see a further pickup in the underlying US
productivity trend to the 2½-3% range and a gradual increase in the US unemployment
rate to 4½%. A backloaded transition is consistent with the projections by equity
analysts that AI investment will keep growing through 2028, albeit at a slower pace than

in 2026-2027. It is also consistent with the renewed pickup in US employment growth
since the spring, which has temporarily laid to rest concerns around imminent AI-related
job market disruption.
1 We characterize the long-run equilibrium level following Hall and Jorgenson (1967) as a function of the
depreciation rate, technological cost disinflation, nominal interest rates, and the AI capital factor share of total
output.
12 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 10

But it is hard to be sure about the timing of this shift and we see some signs that it could 
occur earlier.  Hyperscaler equity price reactions to earnings call signals for higher capex 
switched from positive in 2024-2025 to slightly negative in 2026 as the market has 
become concerned about overinvestment (Exhibit 11). Furthermore, upward revisions to 
hyperscaler capex slowed in the Q3 earnings season, which suggests that the AI 
investment cycle peak is shifting more clearly into view.

### Page 11

Beyond the fundamental demand for compute, we see three other near-term downside 
risks for AI investment: 
Capacity constraints – Semiconductor and memory producers are capacity n
constrained with order backlogs through 2028. While capacity investments currently 
underway should eventually alleviate these constraints, a surge in nominal 
investment in 2027 may increasingly raise prices (thereby pushing central banks 
toward more hawkish policy) without boosting real output. 
Credit markets – Global AI-related debt issuance has totaled $687bn so far in 2026 n
while AI-related equity issuance has added another $115bn. While our market 
strategists see adequate capital supply to meet even higher capital demand next 
year, upward pressure on borrowing costs could lead to more meaningful crowd-out 
of other investment in 2027 (vs. only modest crowd-out in 2026). 
Regulation – Concerns regarding regulatory headwinds to investment surged after n
the US midterm elections in early November raised the possibility of data center 
permitting moratoria in several states (particularly in Texas). We do not anticipate 
that regulation will derail 2027 investment, both because moratoria will likely be 
short-lived and because permits for 2027 projects have mostly already been issued. 
But a more extended delay or stringent regulatory shift would raise downside risks to 
AI investment in 2028-2029, which could weigh on growth in 2027 via tighter 
financial conditions. 
Turning to the labor market, we have argued that AI adoption is likely to displace jobs 
and cause a temporary increase in the unemployment rate of around ½ a percentage 
point over the next several years.2  Our baseline expectation is that this increase occurs 
in a backloaded fashion, with only a small effect in 2027 and more notable effects in 
2028-2029.  However, we are starting to see signs that the AI impact is spreading 
2  See “Revisiting the Extent and Timing of AI Labor Displacement,” Global Economics Analyst, October 22, 
2026.

beyond technology to the broader economy. Across nonmanufacturing industries, the 
share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment (correlations were insignificant at the 
start of the year, Exhibit 12).

Moreover, AI-driven job displacement would likely accelerate in a downside growth 
scenario. Exhibit 13 shows that the trend decline in the employment share of routine 
occupations over the past five decades has been highly concentrated in recessions.  
Given that AI has sharply increased the number of occupations exposed to automation, 
we would expect corporate leaders to respond to a slowdown by reducing headcount 
targets more aggressively, putting more upward pressure on the unemployment rate.
